Metadata is sent in batches, but is not parallelized.\n560: // This is only used for the metadata_config.send setting and 1.x Remote Write.\n561: func (t *QueueManager) AppendWatcherMetadata(ctx context.Context, metadata []scrape.MetricMetadata) {\n562: \t// no op for any newer proto format, which will cache metadata sent to it from the WAL watcher.\n563: \tif t.protoMsg != remoteapi.WriteV1MessageType {\n564: \t\treturn\n565: \t}\n566: \n567: \t// 1.X will still get metadata in batches.\n568: \tmm := make([]prompb.MetricMetadata, 0, len(metadata))\n569: \tfor _, entry := range metadata {\n570: \t\tmm = append(mm, prompb.MetricMetadata{\n571: \t\t\tMetricFamilyName: entry.MetricFamily,\n572: \t\t\tHelp:             entry.Help,\n573: \t\t\tType:             prompb.FromMetadataType(entry.Type),\n574: \t\t\tUnit:             entry.Unit,\n575: \t\t})\n576: \t}\n577: \n578: \tpBuf := proto.NewBuffer(nil)\n579: \tnumSends := int(math.Ceil(float64(len(metadata)) / float64(t.mcfg.MaxSamplesPerSend)))\n580: \tfor i := range numSends {\n581: \t\tlast := min((i+1)*t.mcfg.MaxSamplesPerSend, len(metadata))\n582: \t\terr := \t\t\tt.logger.Error(\"non-recoverable error while sending metadata\", \"count\", last-(i*t.mcfg.MaxSamplesPerSend), \"err\", err)\n586: \t\t}\n587: \t}\n588: }\n589: \n590: func (t *QueueManager) sendMetadataWithBackoff(ctx context.Context, metadata []prompb.MetricMetadata, pBuf *proto.Buffer) error {\n591: \t// Build the WriteRequest with no samples (v1 flow).\n592: \treq, _, _, err := buildWriteRequest(t.logger, nil, metadata, pBuf, nil, nil, t.compr)\n593: \tif err != nil {\n594: \t\treturn err\n595: \t}\n596: \n597: \tmetadataCount := len(metadata)\n598: \n599: \tattemptStore := func(try int) error {\n600: \t\tctx, span := otel.Tracer(\"\").Start(ctx, \"Remote Metadata Send Batch\")\n601: \t\tdefer span.End()\n602: \n603: \t\tspan.SetAttributes(\n604: \t\t\tattribute.Int(\"metadata\", metadataCount),\n605: \t\t\tattribute.Int(\"try\", try),\n606: \t\t\tattribute.String(\"remote_name\", t.storeClient.Name()),\n607: \t\t\tattribute.String(\"remote_url\", t.storeClient.Endpoint()),\n608: \t\t)\n609: \t\t// Attributes defined by OpenTelemetry semantic conventions.\n610: \t\tif try > 0 {\n611: \t\t\tspan.SetAttributes(semconv.HTTPResendCount(try))\n612: \t\t}\n613: \n614: \t\tbegin := time.Now()\n615: \t\t// Ignoring WriteResponseStats, because there is nothing for metadata, since it's\n616: \t\t// embedded in v2 calls now, and we do v1 here.\n617: \t\t_, err := t.storeClient.Store(ctx, req, try)\n618: \t\tt.metrics.sentBatchDuration.Observe(time.Since(begin).Seconds())\n619: \n620: \t\tif err != nil {\n621: \t\t\tspan.RecordError(err)\n622: \t\t\treturn err\n623: \t\t}\n624: \t\treturn nil\n625: \t}\n626: \n627: \tretry := func() {\n628: \t\tt.metrics.retriedMetadataTotal.Add(float64(len(metadata)))\n629: \t}\n630: \terr = t.sendWriteRequestWithBackoff(ctx, attemptStore, retry)\n631: \tif err != nil {\n632: \t\treturn err\n633: \t}\n634: \tt.metrics.metadataTotal.Add(float64(len(metadata)))\n635: \tt.metrics.metadataBytesTotal.Add(float64(len(req)))\n636: \treturn nil\n637: }\n638: \n639: func isSampleOld(baseTime time.Time, sampleAgeLimit time.Duration, ts int64) bool {\n640: \tif sampleAgeLimit == 0 {\n641: \t\t// If sampleAgeLimit is unset, then we never skip samples due to their age.\n642: \t\treturn false\n643: \t}\n644: \tlimitTs := baseTime.Add(-sampleAgeLimit)\n645: \tsampleTs := timestamp.Time(ts)\n646: \treturn sampleTs.Before(limitTs)\n647: }\n648: \n649: // timeSeriesAgeChecker encapsulates the logic for checking if time series data is too old.\n650: type timeSeriesAgeChecker struct {\n651: \tmetrics        *queueManagerMetrics\n652: \tbaseTime       time.Time\n653: \tsampleAgeLimit time.Duration\n654: }\n655: \n656: // checkAndRecordIfOld checks if a timestamp is too old and records the appropriate metric.\n657: // Returns true if the data should be dropped.\n658: func (c *timeSeriesAgeChecker) checkAndRecordIfOld(timestamp int64, dataType string) bool {\n659: \tif c.sampleAgeLimit == 0 {\n660: \t\t// If sampleAgeLimit is unset, then we never skip samples due to their age.\n661: \t\treturn false\n662: \t}\n663: \n664: \tif !isSampleOld(c.baseTime, c.sampleAgeLimit, timestamp) {\n665: \t\treturn false\n666: \t}\n667: \n668: \t// Record the drop in metrics.\n669: \tswitch dataType {\n670: \tcase \"sample\":\n671: \t\tc.metrics.droppedSamplesTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonTooOld).Inc()\n672: \tcase \"histogram\":\n673: \t\tc.metrics.droppedHistogramsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonTooOld).Inc()\n674: \tcase \"exemplar\":\n675: \t\tc.metrics.droppedExemplarsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonTooOld).Inc()\n676: \t}\n677: \treturn true\n678: }\n679: \n680: func isTimeSeriesOldFilter(metrics *queueManagerMetrics, baseTime time.Time, sampleAgeLimit time.Duration) func(ts prompb.TimeSeries) bool {\n681: \tchecker := &timeSeriesAgeChecker{\n682: \t\tmetrics:        metrics,\n683: \t\tbaseTime:       baseTime,\n684: \t\tsampleAgeLimit: sampleAgeLimit,\n685: \t}\n686: \n687: \treturn func(ts prompb.TimeSeries) bool {\n688: \t\t// Only the first element should be set in the series, therefore we only check the first element.\n689: \t\tswitch {\n690: \t\tcase len(ts.Samples) > 0:\n691: \t\t\treturn checker.checkAndRecordIfOld(ts.Samples[0].Timestamp, \"sample\")\n692: \t\tcase len(ts.Histograms) > 0:\n693: \t\t\treturn checker.checkAndRecordIfOld(ts.Histograms[0].Timestamp, \"histogram\")\n694: \t\tcase len(ts.Exemplars) > 0:\n695: \t\t\treturn checker.checkAndRecordIfOld(ts.Exemplars[0].Timestamp, \"exemplar\")\n696: \t\tdefault:\n697: \t\t\treturn false\n698: \t\t}\n699: \t}\n700: }\n701: \n702: func isV2TimeSeriesOldFilter(metrics *queueManagerMetrics, baseTime time.Time, sampleAgeLimit time.Duration) func(ts writev2.TimeSeries) bool {\n703: \tchecker := &timeSeriesAgeChecker{\n704: \t\tmetrics:        metrics,\n705: \t\tbaseTime:       baseTime,\n706: \t\tsampleAgeLimit: sampleAgeLimit,\n707: \t}\n708: \n709: \treturn func(ts writev2.TimeSeries) bool {\n710: \t\t// Only the first element should be set in the series, therefore we only check the first element.\n711: \t\tswitch {\n712: \t\tcase len(ts.Samples) > 0:\n713: \t\t\treturn checker.checkAndRecordIfOld(ts.Samples[0].Timestamp, \"sample\")\n714: \t\tcase len(ts.Histograms) > 0:\n715: \t\t\treturn checker.checkAndRecordIfOld(ts.Histograms[0].Timestamp, \"histogram\")\n716: \t\tcase len(ts.Exemplars) > 0:\n717: \t\t\treturn checker.checkAndRecordIfOld(ts.Exemplars[0].Timestamp, \"exemplar\")\n718: \t\tdefault:\n719: \t\t\treturn false\n720: \t\t}\n721: \t}\n722: }\n723: \n724: // Append queues a sample to be sent to the remote storage. Blocks until all samples are\n725: // enqueued on their shards or a shutdown signal is received.\n726: func (t *QueueManager) Append(samples []record.RefSample) bool {\n727: \tcurrentTime := time.Now()\n728: outer:\n729: \tfor _, s := range samples {\n730: \t\tif isSampleOld(currentTime, time.Duration(t.cfg.SampleAgeLimit), s.T) {\n731: \t\t\tt.metrics.droppedSamplesTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonTooOld).Inc()\n732: \t\t\tcontinue\n733: \t\t}\n734: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n735: \t\tlbls, ok := t.seriesLabels[s.Ref]\n736: \t\tif !ok {\n737: \t\t\tt.dataDropped.incr(1)\n738: \t\t\tif _, ok := t.droppedSeries[s.Ref]; !ok {\n739: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Dropped sample for series that was not explicitly dropped via relabelling\", \"ref\", s.Ref)\n740: \t\t\t\tt.metrics.droppedSamplesTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonUnintentionalDroppedSeries).Inc()\n741: \t\t\t} else {\n742: \t\t\t\tt.metrics.droppedSamplesTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonDroppedSeries).Inc()\n743: \t\t\t}\n744: \t\t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n745: \t\t\tcontinue\n746: \t\t}\n747: \t\t// TODO(cstyan): Handle or at least log an error if no metadata is found.\n748: \t\t// See https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/issues/14405\n749: \t\tmeta := t.seriesMetadata[s.Ref]\n750: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n751: \t\t// Start with a very small backoff. This should not be t.cfg.MinBackoff\n752: \t\t// as it can happen without errors, and we want to pickup work after\n753: \t\t// filling a queue/resharding as quickly as possible.\n754: \t\t// TODO: Consider using the average duration of a request as the backoff.\n755: \t\tbackoff := model.Duration(5 * time.Millisecond)\n756: \t\tfor {\n757: \t\t\tselect {\n758: \t\t\tcase <-t.quit:\n759: \t\t\t\treturn false\n760: \t\t\tdefault:\n761: \t\t\t}\n762: \t\t\tif t.shards.enqueue(s.Ref, timeSeries{\n763: \t\t\t\tseriesLabels: lbls,\n764: \t\t\t\tmetadata:     meta,\n765: \t\t\t\ttimestamp:    s.T,\n766: \t\t\t\tvalue:        s.V,\n767: \t\t\t\tsType:        tSample,\n768: \t\t\t}) {\n769: \t\t\t\tcontinue outer\n770: \t\t\t}\n771: \n772: \t\t\tt.metrics.enqueueRetriesTotal.Inc()\n773: \t\t\ttime.Sleep(time.Duration(backoff))\n774: \t\t\tbackoff *= 2\n775: \t\t\t// It is reasonable to use t.cfg.MaxBackoff here, as if we have hit\n776: \t\t\t// the full backoff we are likely waiting for external resources.\n777: \t\t\tif backoff > t.cfg.MaxBackoff {\n778: \t\t\t\tbackoff = t.cfg.MaxBackoff\n779: \t\t\t}\n780: \t\t}\n781: \t}\n782: \treturn true\n783: }\n784: \n785: func (t *QueueManager) AppendExemplars(exemplars []record.RefExemplar) bool {\n786: \tif !t.sendExemplars {\n787: \t\treturn true\n788: \t}\n789: \tcurrentTime := time.Now()\n790: outer:\n791: \tfor _, e := range exemplars {\n792: \t\tif isSampleOld(currentTime, time.Duration(t.cfg.SampleAgeLimit), e.T) {\n793: \t\t\tt.metrics.droppedExemplarsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonTooOld).Inc()\n794: \t\t\tcontinue\n795: \t\t}\n796: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n797: \t\tlbls, ok := t.seriesLabels[e.Ref]\n798: \t\tif !ok {\n799: \t\t\t// Track dropped exemplars in the same EWMA for sharding calc.\n800: \t\t\tt.dataDropped.incr(1)\n801: \t\t\tif _, ok := t.droppedSeries[e.Ref]; !ok {\n802: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Dropped exemplar for series that was not explicitly dropped via relabelling\", \"ref\", \t\t\t\tt.metrics.droppedExemplarsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonDroppedSeries).Inc()\n806: \t\t\t}\n807: \t\t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n808: \t\t\tcontinue\n809: \t\t}\n810: \t\tmeta := t.seriesMetadata[e.Ref]\n811: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n812: \t\t// This will only loop if the queues are being resharded.\n813: \t\tbackoff := t.cfg.MinBackoff\n814: \t\tfor {\n815: \t\t\tselect {\n816: \t\t\tcase <-t.quit:\n817: \t\t\t\treturn false\n818: \t\t\tdefault:\n819: \t\t\t}\n820: \t\t\tif t.shards.enqueue(e.Ref, timeSeries{\n821: \t\t\t\tseriesLabels:   lbls,\n822: \t\t\t\tmetadata:       meta,\n823: \t\t\t\ttimestamp:      e.T,\n824: \t\t\t\tvalue:          e.V,\n825: \t\t\t\texemplarLabels: e.Labels,\n826: \t\t\t\tsType:          tExemplar,\n827: \t\t\t}) {\n828: \t\t\t\tcontinue outer\n829: \t\t\t}\n830: \n831: \t\t\tt.metrics.enqueueRetriesTotal.Inc()\n832: \t\t\ttime.Sleep(time.Duration(backoff))\n833: \t\t\tbackoff *= 2\n834: \t\t\tif backoff > t.cfg.MaxBackoff {\n835: \t\t\t\tbackoff = t.cfg.MaxBackoff\n836: \t\t\t}\n837: \t\t}\n838: \t}\n839: \treturn true\n840: }\n841: \n842: func (t *QueueManager) AppendHistograms(histograms []record.RefHistogramSample) bool {\n843: \tif !t.sendNativeHistograms {\n844: \t\treturn true\n845: \t}\n846: \tcurrentTime := time.Now()\n847: outer:\n848: \tfor _, h := range histograms {\n849: does not support itB\", \"ref\", h.Ref)\n857: \t\t\tcontinue\n858: \t\t}\n859: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n860: \t\tlbls, ok := t.seriesLabels[h.Ref]\n861: \t\tif !ok {\n862: \t\t\t\tt.metrics.droppedHistogramsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonDroppedSeries).Inc()\n868: \t\t\t}\n869: \t\t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n870: \t\t\tcontinue\n871: \t\t}\n872: \t\tmeta := t.seriesMetadata[h.Ref]\n873: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n874: \n875: \t\tbackoff := model.Duration(5 * time.Millisecond)\n876: \t\tfor {\n877: \t\t\tselect {\n878: \t\t\tcase <-t.quit:\n879: \t\t\t\treturn false\n880: \t\t\tdefault:\n881: \t\t\t}\n882: \t\t\tif t.shards.enqueue(h.Ref, timeSeries{\n883: \t\t\t\tseriesLabels: lbls,\n884: \t\t\t\tmetadata:     meta,\n885: \t\t\t\ttimestamp:    h.T,\n886: \t\t\t\thistogram:    h.H,\n887: \t\t\t\tsType:        tHistogram,\n888: \t\t\t}) {\n889: \t\t\t\tcontinue outer\n890: \t\t\t}\n891: \n892: \t\t\tt.metrics.enqueueRetriesTotal.Inc()\n893: \t\t\ttime.Sleep(time.Duration(backoff))\n894: \t\t\tbackoff *= 2\n895: \t\t\tif backoff > t.cfg.MaxBackoff {\n896: \t\t\t\tbackoff = t.cfg.MaxBackoff\n897: \t\t\t}\n898: \t\t}\n899: \t}\n900: \treturn true\n901: }\n902: \n903: func (t *QueueManager) AppendFloatHistograms(floatHistograms []record.RefFloatHistogramSample) bool {\n904: \tif !t.sendNativeHistograms {\n905: \t\treturn true\n906: \t}\n907: \tcurrentTime := time.Now()\n908: outer:\n909: \tfor _, h := range floatHistograms {\n910: \t\tif isSampleOld(currentTime, time.Duration(t.cfg.SampleAgeLimit), h.T) {\n911: \t\t\tt.metrics.droppedHistogramsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonTooOld).Inc()\n912: \t\t\tcontinue\n913: \t\t}\n914: \t\tif t.protoMsg == remoteapi.WriteV1MessageType && h.FH != nil && h.FH.Schema == histogram.CustomBucketsSchema {\n915: \t\t\t// We cannot send native histograms with custom buckets (NHCB) via remote write v1.\n916: \t\t\tt.metrics.droppedHistogramsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonNHCBNotSupported).Inc()\n917: \t\t\tt.logger.Warn(\"Dropped float native histogram with custom buckets (NHCB) as remote write v1 does not support itB\", \"ref\", h.Ref)\n918: \t\t\tcontinue\n919: \t\t}\n920: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n921: \t\tlbls, ok := t.seriesLabels[h.Ref]\n922: \t\tif !ok {\n923: \t\t\tt.dataDropped.incr(1)\n924: \t\t\tif _, ok := t.droppedSeries[h.Ref]; !ok {\n925: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Dropped histogram for series that was not explicitly dropped via relabelling\", \"ref\", h.Ref)\n926: \t\t\t\tt.metrics.droppedHistogramsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonUnintentionalDroppedSeries).Inc()\n927: \t\t\t} else {\n928: \t\t\t\tt.metrics.droppedHistogramsTotal.WithLabelValues(reasonDroppedSeries).Inc()\n929: \t\t\t}\n930: \t\t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n931: \t\t\tcontinue\n932: \t\t}\n933: \t\tmeta := t.seriesMetadata[h.Ref]\n934: \t\tt.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n935: \n936: \t\tbackoff := model.Duration(5 * time.Millisecond)\n937: \t\tfor {\n938: \t\t\tselect {\n939: \t\t\tcase <-t.quit:\n940: \t\t\t\treturn false\n941: \t\t\tdefault:\n942: \t\t\t}\n943: \t\t\tif t.shards.enqueue(h.Ref, timeSeries{\n944: \t\t\t\tseriesLabels:   lbls,\n945: \t\t\t\tmetadata:       meta,\n946: \t\t\t\ttimestamp:      h.T,\n947: \t\t\t\tfloatHistogram: h.FH,\n948: \t\t\t\tsType:          tFloatHistogram,\n949: \t\t\t}) {\n950: \t\t\t\tcontinue outer\n951: \t\t\t}\n952: \n953: \t\t\tt.metrics.enqueueRetriesTotal.Inc()\n954: \t\t\ttime.Sleep(time.Duration(backoff))\n955: \t\t\tbackoff *= 2\n956: \t\t\tif backoff > t.cfg.MaxBackoff {\n957: \t\t\t\tbackoff = t.cfg.MaxBackoff\n958: \t\t\t}\n959: \t\t}\n960: \t}\n961: \treturn true\n962: }\n963: \n964: // Start the queue manager sending samples to the remote storage.\n965: // Does not block.\n966: func (t *QueueManager) Start() {\n967: \t// Register and initialise some metrics.\n968: \tt.metrics.register()\n969: \tt.metrics.shardCapacity.Set(float64(t.cfg.Capacity))\n970: \tt.metrics.maxNumShards.Set(float64(t.cfg.MaxShards))\n971: \tt.metrics.minNumShards.Set(float64(t.cfg.MinShards))\n972: \tt.metrics.desiredNumShards.Set(float64(t.cfg.MinShards))\n973: \tt.metrics.maxSamplesPerSend.Set(float64(t.cfg.MaxSamplesPerSend))\n974: \n975: \tt.shards.start(t.numShards)\n976: \tt.watcher.Start()\n977: \tif t.mcfg.Send {\n978: \t\tt.metadataWatcher.Start()\n979: \t}\n980: \n981: \tt.wg.Add(2)\n982: \tgo t.updateShardsLoop()\n983: \tgo t.reshardLoop()\n984: }\n985: \n986: // Stop stops sending samples to the remote storage and waits for pending\n987: // sends to complete.\n988: func (t *QueueManager) Stop() {\n989: \tt.logger.Info(\"Stopping remote storage...\")\n990: \tdefer t.logger.Info(\"Remote storage stopped.\")\n991: \n992: \tclose(t.quit)\n993: \t// Wait for all QueueManager routines to end before stopping shards, metadata watcher, and WAL watcher. This\n994: \t// is to ensure we don't end up executing a reshard and shards.stop() at the same time, which\n995: \t// causes a closed channel panic.\n996: \tt.wg.Wait()\n997: \tt.shards.stop()\n998: \tt.watcher.Stop()\n999: \tif t.mcfg.Send {\n1000: \t\tt.metadataWatcher.Stop()\n1001: \t}\n1002: \tt.metrics.unregister()\n1003: }\n1004: \n1005: // StoreSeries keeps track of which series we know about for lookups when sending samples to remote.\n1006: func (t *QueueManager) StoreSeries(series []record.RefSeries, index int) {\n1007: \tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n1008: \tdefer t.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n1009: \tt.seriesSegmentMtx.Lock()\n1010: \tdefer t.seriesSegmentMtx.Unlock()\n1011: \tfor _, s := range series {\n1012: \t\t// Just make sure all the Refs of Series will insert into seriesSegmentIndexes map for tracking.\n1013: \t\tt.seriesSegmentIndexes[s.Ref] = index\n1014: \n1015: \t\tt.builder.Reset(s.Labels)\n1016: \t\tprocessExternalLabels(t.builder, t.externalLabels)\n1017: \t\tkeep := relabel.ProcessBuilder(t.builder, t.relabelConfigs...)\n1018: \t\tif !keep {\n1019: \t\t\tt.droppedSeries[s.Ref] = struct{}{}\n1020: \t\t\tcontinue\n1021: \t\t}\n1022: \t\tlbls := t.builder.Labels()\n1023: \t\tt.seriesLabels[s.Ref] = lbls\n1024: \t}\n1025: }\n1026: \n1027: // StoreMetadata keeps track of known series' metadata for lookups when sending samples to remote.\n1028: func (t *QueueManager) StoreMetadata(meta []record.RefMetadata) {\n1029: \tif t.protoMsg == remoteapi.WriteV1MessageType {\n1030: \t\treturn\n1031: \t}\n1032: \n1033: \tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n1034: \tdefer t.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n1035: \tfor _, m := range meta {\n1036: \t\tt.seriesMetadata[m.Ref] = &metadata.Metadata{\n1037: \t\t\tType: record.ToMetricType(m.Type),\n1038: \t\t\tUnit: m.Unit,\n1039: \t\t\tHelp: m.Help,\n1040: \t\t}\n1041: \t}\n1042: }\n1043: \n1044: // UpdateSeriesSegment updates the segment number held against the series,\n1045: // so we can trim older ones in SeriesReset.\n1046: func (t *QueueManager) UpdateSeriesSegment(series []record.RefSeries, index int) {\n1047: \tt.seriesSegmentMtx.Lock()\n1048: \tdefer t.seriesSegmentMtx.Unlock()\n1049: \tfor _, s := range series {\n1050: \t\tt.seriesSegmentIndexes[s.Ref] = index\n1051: \t}\n1052: }\n1053: \n1054: // SeriesReset is used when reading a checkpoint. WAL Watcher should have\n1055: // stored series records with the checkpoints index number, so we can now\n1056: // delete any ref ID's lower than that # from the two maps.\n1057: func (t *QueueManager) SeriesReset(index int) {\n1058: \tt.seriesMtx.Lock()\n1059: \tdefer t.seriesMtx.Unlock()\n1060: \tt.seriesSegmentMtx.Lock()\n1061: \tdefer t.seriesSegmentMtx.Unlock()\n1062: \t// Check for series that are in segments older than the checkpoint\n1063: \t// that were not also present in the checkpoint.\n1064: \tfor k, v := range t.seriesSegmentIndexes {\n1065: \t\tif v < index {\n1066: \t\t\tdelete(t.seriesSegmentIndexes, k)\n1067: \t\t\tdelete(t.seriesLabels, k)\n1068: \t\t\tdelete(t.seriesMetadata, k)\n1069: \t\t\tdelete(t.droppedSeries, k)\n1070: \t\t}\n1071: \t}\n1072: }\n1073: \n1074: // SetClient updates the client used by a queue. Used when only client specific\n1075: // fields are updated to avoid restarting the queue.\n1076: func (t *QueueManager) SetClient(c WriteClient) {\n1077: \tt.clientMtx.Lock()\n1078: \tt.storeClient = c\n1079: \tt.clientMtx.Unlock()\n1080: }\n1081: \n1082: func (t *QueueManager) client() WriteClient {\n1083: \tt.clientMtx.RLock()\n1084: \tdefer t.clientMtx.RUnlock()\n1085: \treturn t.storeClient\n1086: }\n1087: \n1088: // processExternalLabels merges externalLabels into b. If b contains\n1089: // a label in externalLabels, the value in b wins.\n1090: func processExternalLabels(b *labels.Builder, externalLabels []labels.Label) {\n1091: \tfor _, el := range externalLabels {\n1092: \t\tif b.Get(el.Name) == \"\" {\n1093: \t\t\tb.Set(el.Name, el.Value)\n1094: \t\t}\n1095: \t}\n1096: }\n1097: \n1098: func (t *QueueManager) updateShardsLoop() {\n1099: \tdefer t.wg.Done()\n1100: \n1101: \tticker := time.NewTicker(shardUpdateDuration)\n1102: \tdefer ticker.Stop()\n1103: \tfor {\n1104: \t\tselect {\n1105: \t\tcase <-ticker.C:\n1106: \t\t\tdesiredShards := t.calculateDesiredShards()\n1107: \t\t\tif !t.shouldReshard(desiredShards) {\n1108: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n1109: \t\t\t}\n1110: \t\t\t// Resharding can take some time, and we want this loop\n1111: \t\t\t// to stay close to shardUpdateDuration.\n1112: \t\t\tselect {\n1113: \t\t\tcase t.reshardChan <- desiredShards:\n1114: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Remote storage resharding\", \"from\", t.numShards, \"to\", desiredShards)\n1115: \t\t\t\tt.numShards = desiredShards\n1116: \t\t\tdefault:\n1117: \t\t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Currently resharding, skipping.\")\n1118: \t\t\t}\n1119: \t\tcase <-t.quit:\n1120: \t\t\treturn\n1121: \t\t}\n1122: \t}\n1123: }\n1124: \n1125: // shouldReshard returns whether resharding should occur.\n1126: func (t *QueueManager) shouldReshard(desiredShards int) bool {\n1127: \tif desiredShards == t.numShards {\n1128: \t\treturn false\n1129: \t}\n1130: \t// We shouldn't reshard if Prometheus hasn't been able to send\n1131: \t// since the last time it checked if it should reshard.\n1132: \tminSendTimestamp := time.Now().Add(-1 * shardUpdateDuration).Unix()\n1133: \tlsts := t.lastSendTimestamp.Load()\n1134: \tif lsts < minSendTimestamp {\n1135: \t\tt.logger.Warn(\"Skipping resharding, last successful send was beyond threshold\", \"lastSendTimestamp\", lsts, \"minSendTimestamp\", minSendTimestamp)\n1136: \t\treturn false\n1137: \t}\n1138: \tif disableTimestamp := t.reshardDisableEndTimestamp.Load(); time.Now().Unix() < disableTimestamp {\n1139: \t\tdisabledAt := time.Unix(t.reshardDisableStartTimestamp.Load(), 0)\n1140: \t\tdisabledFor := time.Until(time.Unix(disableTimestamp, 0))\n1141: \n1142: \t\tt.logger.Warn(\"Skipping resharding, resharding is disabled while waiting for recoverable errors\", \"disabled_at\", disabledAt, \"disabled_for\", disabledFor)\n1143: \t\treturn false\n1144: \t}\n1145: \treturn true\n1146: }\n1147: \n1148: // calculateDesiredShards returns the number of desired shards, which will be\n1149: // the current QueueManager.numShards if resharding should not occur for reasons\n1150: // outlined in this functions implementation. It is up to the caller to reshard, or not,\n1151: // based on the return value.\n1152: func (t *QueueManager) calculateDesiredShards() int {\n1153: \tt.dataOut.tick()\n1154: \tt.dataDropped.tick()\n1155: \tt.dataOutDuration.tick()\n1156: \n1157: \t// We use the number of incoming samples as a prediction of how much work we\n1158: \t// will need to do next iteration.  We add to this any pending samples\n1159: \t// (received - send) so we can catch up with any backlog. We use the average\n1160: \t// outgoing batch latency to work out how many shards we need.\n1161: \tvar (\n1162: \t\tdataInRate      = t.dataIn.rate()\n1163: \t\tdataOutRate     = t.dataOut.rate()\n1164: \t\tdataKeptRatio   = dataOutRate / (t.dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)\n1165: \t\tdataOutDuration = t.dataOutDuration.rate() / float64(time.Second)\n1166: \t\tdataPendingRate = dataInRate*dataKeptRatio - dataOutRate\n1167: \t\thighestSent     = t.metrics.highestSentTimestamp.Get()\n1168: \t\thighestRecv     = t.highestRecvTimestamp.Get()\n1169: \t\tdelay           = highestRecv - highestSent\n1170: \t\tdataPending     = delay * dataInRate * dataKeptRatio\n1171: \t)\n1172: \n1173: \tif dataOutRate <= 0 {\n1174: \t\treturn t.numShards\n1175: \t}\n1176: \n1177: \tvar (\n1178: \t\t// When behind we will try to catch up on 5% of samples per second.\n1179: \t\tbacklogCatchup = 0.05 * dataPending\n1180: \t\t// Calculate Time to send one sample, averaged across all sends done this tick.\n1181: \t\ttimePerSample = dataOutDuration / dataOutRate\n1182: \t\tdesiredShards = timePerSample * (dataInRate*dataKeptRatio + backlogCatchup)\n1183: \t)\n1184: \tt.metrics.desiredNumShards.Set(desiredShards)\n1185: \tt.logger.Debug(\"QueueManager.calculateDesiredShards\",\n1186: \t\t\"dataInRate\", dataInRate,\n1187: \t\t\"dataOutRate\", dataOutRate,\n1188: \t\t\"dataKeptRatio\", dataKeptRatio,\n1189: \t\t\"dataPendingRate\", dataPendingRate,\n1190: \t\t\"dataPending\", dataPending,\n1191: \t\t\"dataOutDuration\", dataOutDuration,\n1192: \t\t\"timePerSample\", timePerSample,\n1193: \t\t\"desiredShards\", desiredShards,\n1194: \t\t\"highestSent\", highestSent,\n1195: \t\t\"highestRecv\", highestRecv,\n1196: \t)\n1197: \n1198: \t// Changes in the number of shards must be greater than shardToleranceFraction.\n1199: \tvar (\n1200: \t\tlowerBound = float64(t.numShards) * (1. - shardToleranceFraction)\n1201: \t\tupperBound = float64(t.numShards) * (1. + shardToleranceFraction)\n1202: \t)\n1203: \tt.logger.Debug(\"QueueManager.updateShardsLoop\",\n1204: \t\t\"lowerBound\", lowerBound, \"desiredShards\", desiredShards, \"upperBound\", upperBound)\n1205: \n1206: \tdesiredShards = math.Ceil(desiredShards) // Round up to be on the safe side.\n1207: \tif lowerBound <= desiredShards && desiredShards <= upperBound {\n1208: \t\treturn t.numShards\n1209: \t}\n1210: \n1211: \tnumShards := int(desiredShards)\n1212: \t// Do not downshard if we are more than ten seconds back.\n1213: \tif numShards < t.numShards && delay > 10.0 {\n1214: \t\tt.logger.Debug(\"Not downsharding due to being too far behind\")\n1215: \t\treturn t.numShards\n1216: \t}\n1217: \n1218: \tswitch {\n1219: \tcase numShards > t.cfg.MaxShards:\n1220: \t\tnumShards = t.cfg.MaxShards\n1221: \tcase numShards < t.cfg.MinShards:\n1222: \t\tnumShards = t.cfg.MinShards\n1223: \t}\n1224: \treturn numShards\n1225: }\n1226: \n1227: func (t *QueueManager) reshardLoop() {\n1228: \tdefer t.wg.Done()\n1229: \n1230: \tfor {\n1231: \t\tselect {\n1232: \t\tcase numShards := <-t.reshardChan:\n1233: \t\t\t// We start the newShards after we have stopped (the therefore completely\n1234: \t\t\t// flushed) the oldShards, to guarantee we only every deliver samples in\n1235: \t\t\t// order.\n1236: \t\t\tt.shards.stop()\n1237: \t\t\tt.shards.start(numShards)\n1238: \t\tcase <-t.quit:\n1239: \t\t\treturn\n1240: \t\t}\n1241: \t}\n1242: }\n1243: \n1244: func (t *QueueManager) newShards() *shards {\n1245: \ts := &shards{\n1246: \t\tqm:   t,\n1247: \t\tdone: make(chan struct{}),\n1248: \t}\n1249: \treturn s\n1250: }\n1251: \n1252: type shards struct {\n1253: \tmtx sync.RWMutex // With the WAL, this is never actually contended.\n1254: \n1255: \tqm     *QueueManager\n1256: \tqueues []*queue\n1257: \t// So we can accurately track how many of each are lost during shard shutdowns.\n1258: \tenqueuedSamples    atomic.Int64\n1259: \tenqueuedExemplars  atomic.Int64\n1260: \tenqueuedHistograms atomic.Int64\n1261: \n1262: \t// Emulate a wait group with a channel and an atomic int, as you\n1263: \t// cannot select on a wait group.\n1264: \tdone    chan struct{}\n1265: \trunning atomic.Int32\n1266: \n1267: \t// Soft shutdown context will prevent new enqueues and deadlocks.\n1268: \tsoftShutdown chan struct{}\n1269: \n1270: \t// Hard shutdown context is used to terminate outgoing HTTP connections\n1271: \t// after giving them a chance to terminate.\n1272: \thardShutdown                    context.CancelFunc\n1273: \tsamplesDroppedOnHardShutdown    atomic.Uint32\n1274: \texemplarsDroppedOnHardShutdown  atomic.Uint32\n1275: \thistogramsDroppedOnHardShutdown atomic.Uint32\n1276: \tmetadataDroppedOnHardShutdown   atomic.Uint32\n1277: }\n1278: \n1279: // start the shards; must be called before any call to enqueue.\n1280: func (s *shards) start(n int) {\n1281: \ts.mtx.Lock()\n1282: \tdefer s.mtx.Unlock()\n1283: \n1284: \ts.qm.metrics.pendingSamples.Set(0)\n1285: \ts.qm.metrics.numShards.Set(float64(n))\n1286: \n1287: \tnewQueues := make([]*queue, n)\n1288: \tfor i := range n {\n1289: \t\tnewQueues[i] = newQueue(s.qm.cfg.MaxSamplesPerSend, s.qm.cfg.Capacity)\n1290: \t}\n1291: \n1292: \ts.queues = newQueues\n1293: \n1294: \tvar hardShutdownCtx context.Context\n1295: \thardShutdownCtx, s.hardShutdown = context.WithCancel(context.Background())\n1296: \ts.softShutdown = make(chan struct{})\n1297: \ts.running.Store(int32(n))\n1298: \ts.done = make(chan struct{})\n1299: \ts.enqueuedSamples.Store(0)\n1300: \ts.enqueuedExemplars.Store(0)\n1301: \ts.enqueuedHistograms.Store(0)\n1302: \ts.samplesDroppedOnHardShutdown.Store(0)\n1303: \ts.exemplarsDroppedOnHardShutdown.Store(0)\n1304: \ts.histogramsDroppedOnHardShutdown.Store(0)\n1305: \ts.metadataDroppedOnHardShutdown.Store(0)\n1306: \tfor i := range n {\n1307: \t\tgo s.runShard(hardShutdownCtx, i, newQueues[i])\n1308: \t}\n1309: }\n1310: \n1311: // stop the shards; subsequent call to enqueue will return false.\n1312: func (s *shards) stop() {\n1313: \t// Attempt a clean shutdown, but only wait flushDeadline for all the shards\n1314: \t// to cleanly exit. As we're doing RPCs, enqueue can block indefinitely.\n1315: \t// We must be able so call stop concurrently, hence we can only take the\n1316: \t// RLock here.\n1317: \ts.mtx.RLock()\n1318: \tclose(s.softShutdown)\n1319: \ts.mtx.RUnlock()\n1320: \n1321: \t// Enqueue should now be unblocked, so we can take the write lock.  This\n1322: \t// also ensures we don't race with writes to the queues, and get a panic:\n1323: \t// send on closed channel.\n1324: \ts.mtx.Lock()\n1325: \tdefer s.mtx.Unlock()\n1326: \tfor _, queue := range s.queues {\n1327: \t\tgo queue.FlushAndShutdown(s.done)\n1328: \t}\n1329: \tselect {\n1330: \tcase <-s.done:\n1331: \t\treturn\n1332: \tcase <-time.After(s.qm.flushDeadline):\n1333: \t}\n1334: \n1335: \t// Force an unclean shutdown.\n1336: \ts.hardShutdown()\n1337: \t<-s.done\n1338: \n1339: \t// Log error for any dropped samples, exemplars, or histograms.\n1340: \tlogDroppedError := func(t string, counter atomic.Uint32) {\n1341: \t\tif dropped := counter.Load(); dropped > 0 {\n1342: \t\t\ts.qm.logger.Error(fmt.Sprintf(\"Failed to flush all %s on shutdown\", t), \"count\", dropped)\n1343: \t\t}\n1344: \t}\n1345: \tlogDroppedError(\"samples\", s.samplesDroppedOnHardShutdown)\n1346: \tlogDroppedError(\"exemplars\", s.exemplarsDroppedOnHardShutdown)\n1347: \tlogDroppedError(\"histograms\", s.histogramsDroppedOnHardShutdown)\n1348: }\n1349: \n1350: // enqueue data (sample or exemplar). If the shard is full, shutting down, or\n1351: // resharding, it will return false; in this case, you should back off and\n1352: // retry. A shard is full when its configured capacity has been reached,\n1353: // specifically, when s.queues[shard] has filled its batchQueue channel and the\n1354: // partial batch has also been filled.\n1355: func (s *shards) enqueue(ref chunks.HeadSeriesRef, data timeSeries) bool {\n1356: \ts.mtx.RLock()\n1357: \tdefer s.mtx.RUnlock()\n1358: \tshard := uint64(ref) % uint64(len(s.queues))\n1359: \tselect {\n1360: \tcase <-s.softShutdown:\n1361: \t\treturn false\n1362: \tdefault:\n1363: \t\tappended := s.queues[shard].Append(data)\n1364: \t\tif !appended {\n1365: \t\t\treturn false\n1366: \t\t}\n1367: \t\tswitch data.sType {\n1368: \t\tcase tSample:\n1369: \t\t\ts.qm.metrics.pendingSamples.Inc()\n1370: \t\t\ts.enqueuedSamples.Inc()\n1371: \t\tcase tExemplar:\n1372: \t\t\ts.qm.metrics.pendingExemplars.Inc()\n1373: \t\t\ts.enqueuedExemplars.Inc()\n1374: \t\tcase tHistogram, tFloatHistogram:\n1375: \t\t\ts.qm.metrics.pendingHistograms.Inc()\n1376: \t\t\ts.enqueuedHistograms.Inc()\n1377: \t\tdefault:\n1378: \t\t\treturn true\n1379: \t\t}\n1380: \t\ts.qm.metrics.highestTimestamp.Set(float64(data.timestamp / 1000))\n1381: \t\treturn true\n1382: \t}\n1383: }\n1384: \n1385: type queue struct {\n1386: \t// batchMtx covers operations appending to or publishing the partial batch.\n1387: \tbatchMtx   sync.Mutex\n1388: \tbatch      []timeSeries\n1389: \tbatchQueue chan []timeSeries\n1390: \n1391: \t// Since we know there are a limited number of batches out, using a stack\n1392: \t// is easy and safe so a sync.Pool is not necessary.\n1393: \t// poolMtx covers adding and removing batches from the batchPool.\n1394: \tpoolMtx   sync.Mutex\n1395: \tbatchPool [][]timeSeries\n1396: }\n1397: \n1398: type timeSeries struct {\n1399: \tseriesLabels   labels.Labels\n1400: \tvalue          float64\n1401: \thistogram      *histogram.Histogram\n1402: \tfloatHistogram *histogram.FloatHistogram\n1403: \tmetadata       *metadata.Metadata\n1404: \ttimestamp      int64\n1405: \texemplarLabels labels.Labels\n1406: \t// The type of series: sample, exemplar, or histogram.\n1407: \tsType seriesType\n1408: }\n1409: \n1410: type seriesType int\n1411: \n1412: const (\n1413: \ttSample seriesType = iota\n1414: \ttExemplar\n1415: \ttHistogram\n1416: \ttFloatHistogram\n1417: \ttMetadata\n1418: )\n1419: \n1420: func newQueue(batchSize, capacity int) *queue {\n1421: \tbatches := capacity / batchSize\n1422: \t// Always create an unbuffered channel even if capacity is configured to be\n1423: \t// less than max_samples_per_send.\n1424: \tif batches == 0 {\n1425: \t\tbatches = 1\n1426: \t}\n1427: \treturn &queue{\n1428: \t\tbatch:      make([]timeSeries, 0, batchSize),\n1429: \t\tbatchQueue: make(chan []timeSeries, batches),\n1430: \t\t// batchPool should have capacity for everything in the channel + 1 for\n1431: \t\t// the batch being processed.\n1432: \t\tbatchPool: make([][]timeSeries, 0, batches+1),\n1433: \t}\n1434: }\n1435: \n1436: // Append the timeSeries to the buffered batch. Returns false if it\n1437: // cannot be added and must be retried.\n1438: func (q *queue) Append(datum timeSeries) bool {\n1439: \tq.batchMtx.Lock()\n1440: \tdefer q.batchMtx.Unlock()\n1441: \t// TODO(cstyan): Check if metadata now means we've reduced the total # of samples\n1442: \t// we can batch together here, and if so find a way to not include metadata\n1443: \t// in the batch size calculation.\n1444: \t// See https://github.com/prometheus/prometheus/issues/14405\n1445: \tq.batch = append(q.batch, datum)\n1446: \tif len(q.batch) == cap(q.batch) {\n1447: \t\tselect {\n1448: \t\tcase q.batchQueue <- q.batch:\n1449: \t\t\tq.batch = q.newBatch(cap(q.batch))\n1450: \t\t\treturn true\n1451: \t\tdefault:\n1452: \t\t\t// Remove the sample we just appended. It will get retried.\n1453: \t\t\tq.batch = q.batch[:len(q.batch)-1]\n1454: \t\t\treturn false\n1455: \t\t}\n1456: \t}\n1457: \treturn true\n1458: }\n1459: \n1460: func (q *queue) Chan() <-chan []timeSeries {\n1461: \treturn q.batchQueue\n1462: }\n1463: \n1464: // Batch returns the current batch and allocates a new batch.\n1465: func (q *queue) Batch() []timeSeries {\n1466: \tq.batchMtx.Lock()\n1467: \tdefer q.batchMtx.Unlock()\n1468: \tselect {\n1469: \tcase batch := <-q.batchQueue:\n1470: \t\treturn batch\n1471: \tdefault:\n1472: \t\tbatch := q.batch\n1473: \t\tq.batch = q.newBatch(cap(batch))\n1474: \t\treturn batch\n1475: \t}\n1476: }\n1477: \n1478: // ReturnForReuse adds the batch buffer back to the internal pool.\n1479: func (q *queue) ReturnForReuse(batch []timeSeries) {\n1480: \tq.poolMtx.Lock()\n1481: \tdefer q.poolMtx.Unlock()\n1482: \tif len(q.batchPool) < cap(q.batchPool) {\n1483: \t\tq.batchPool = append(q.batchPool, batch[:0])\n1484: \t}\n1485: }\n1486: \n1487: // FlushAndShutdown stops the queue and flushes any samples. No appends can be\n1488: // made after this is called.\n1489: func (q *queue) FlushAndShutdown(done <-chan struct{}) {\n1490: loop:\n1491: \tfor q.tryEnqueueingBatch(done) {\n1492: \t\tselect {\n1493: \t\tcase <-done:\n1494: \t\t\tbreak loop\n1495: \t\tcase <-time.After(time.Second):\n1496: \t\t}\n1497: \t}\n1498: \n1499: \tq.batchMtx.Lock()\n1500: \tdefer q.batchMtx.Unlock()\n1501: \tq.batch = nil\n1502: \tclose(q.batchQueue)\n1503: }\n1504: \n1505: // tryEnqueueingBatch tries to send a batch if necessary. If sending needs to\n1506: // be retried it will return true.\n1507: func (q *queue) tryEnqueueingBatch(done <-chan struct{}) bool {\n1508: \tq.batchMtx.Lock()\n1509: \tdefer q.batchMtx.Unlock()\n1510: \tif len(q.batch) == 0 {\n1511: \t\treturn false\n1512: \t}\n1513: \n1514: \tselect {\n1515: \tcase q.batchQueue <- q.batch:\n1516: \t\treturn false\n1517: \tcase <-done:\n1518: \t\t// The shard has been hard shut down, so no more samples can be sent.\n1519: \t\t// No need to try again as we will drop everything left in the queue.\n1520: \t\treturn false\n1521: \tdefault:\n1522: \t\t// The batchQueue is full, so we need to try again later.\n1523: \t\treturn true\n1524: \t}\n1525: }\n1526: \n1527: func (q *queue) newBatch(capacity int) []timeSeries {\n1528: \tq.poolMtx.Lock()\n1529: \tdefer q.poolMtx.Unlock()\n1530: \tbatches := len(q.batchPool)\n1531: \tif batches > 0 {\n1532: \t\tbatch := q.batchPool[batches-1]\n1533: \t\tq.batchPool = q.batchPool[:batches-1]\n1534: \t\treturn batch\n1535: \t}\n1536: \treturn make([]timeSeries, 0, capacity)\n1537: }\n1538: \n1539: func (s *shards) runShard(ctx context.Context, shardID int, queue *queue) {\n1540: \tdefer func() {\n1541: \t\tif s.running.Dec() == 0 {\n1542: \t\t\tclose(s.done)\n1543: \t\t}\n1544: \t}()\n1545: \n1546: \tshardNum := strconv.Itoa(shardID)\n1547: \tsymbolTable := \n1652: func populateTimeSeries(batch []timeSeries, pendingData []prompb.TimeSeries, sendExemplars, sendNativeHistograms bool) (int, int, int) {\n1653: \tvar nPendingSamples, nPendingExemplars, WriteResponseStats, duration time.Duration) {\n1710: \t// Partial errors may happen -- account for that.\n1711: \tsampleDiff := sampleCount - rs.Samples\n1712: \tif sampleDiff > 0 {\n1713: func(int) error, onRetry func()) error {\n2023: \tbackoff := t.cfg.MinBackoff\n2024: \tsleepDuration := model.Duration(0)\n2025: \ttry := 0\n2026: \n2027: \tfor {\n2028: \t\tselect {\n2029: \t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n2030: \t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n2031: \t\tdefault:\n2032: \t\t}\n2033: \n2034: \t\terr := attempt(try)\n2035: \n2036: \t\tif err == nil {\n2037: \t\t\treturn nil\n2038: \t\t}\n2039: \n2040: \t\t// If the error is unrecoverable, we should not retry.\n2041: \t\tvar backoffErr RecoverableError\n2042: \t\tif !errors.As(err, &backoffErr) {\n2043: \t\t\treturn err\n2044: \t\t}\n2045: \n2046: \t\tsleepDuration = backoff\n2047: \t\tswitch {\n2048: \t\tcase backoffErr.retryAfter > 0:\n2049: \t\t\tsleepDuration = backoffErr.retryAfter\n2050: \t\t\tt.logger.Info(\"Retrying after duration specified by Retry-After header\", \"duration\", sleepDuration)\n2051: \t\tcase backoffErr.retryAfter < 0:\n2052: \t\t\tt.logger.Debug(\"retry-after cannot be in past, retrying using default backoff mechanism\")\n2053: \t\t}\n2054: \n2055: \t\t// We should never reshard for a recoverable error; increasing shards could\n2056: \t\t// make the problem worse, particularly if we're getting rate limited.\n2057: \t\t//\n2058: \t\t// reshardDisableTimestamp holds the unix timestamp until which resharding\n2059: \t\t// is disabled. We'll update that timestamp if the period we were just told\n2060: \t\t// to sleep for is newer than the existing disabled timestamp.\n2061: \t\treshardWaitPeriod := time.Now().Add(time.Duration(sleepDuration) * 2)\n2062: \t\tif oldTS, updated := setAtomicToNewer(&t.reshardDisableEndTimestamp, reshardWaitPeriod.Unix()); updated {\n2063: \t\t\t// If the old timestamp was in the past, then resharding was previously\n2064: \t\t\t// enabled. We want to track the time where it initially got disabled for\n2065: \t\t\t// logging purposes.\n2066: \t\t\tdisableTime := time.Now().Unix()\n2067: \t\t\tif oldTS < disableTime {\n2068: \t\t\t\tt.reshardDisableStartTimestamp.Store(disableTime)\n2069: \t\t\t}\n2070: \t\t}\n2071: \n2072: \t\tselect {\n2073: \t\tcase <-ctx.Done():\n2074: \t\tcase <-time.After(time.Duration(sleepDuration)):\n2075: \t\t}\n2076: \n2077: \t\t// If we make it this far, we've encountered a recoverable error and will retry.\n2078: \t\tonRetry()\n2079: \t\tt.logger.Warn(\"Failed to send batch, retrying\", \"err\", err)\n2080: \n2081: \t\tbackoff = min(sleepDuration*2, t.cfg.MaxBackoff)\n2082: \n2083: \t\ttry++\n2084: \t}\n2085: }\n2086: \n2087: // setAtomicToNewer atomically sets a value to the newer int64 between itself\n2088: // and the provided newValue argument. setAtomicToNewer returns whether the\n2089: // atomic value was updated and what the previous value was.\n2090: func setAtomicToNewer(value *atomic.Int64, newValue int64) (previous int64, updated bool) {\n2091: \tfor {\n2092: \t\tcurrent := value.Load()\n2093: \t\tif current >= newValue {\n2094: \t\t\t// If the current stored value is newer than newValue; abort.\n2095: \t\t\treturn current, false\n2096: \t\t}\n2097: \n2098: \t\t// Try to swap the value. If the atomic value has changed, we loop back to\n2099: \t\t// the beginning until we've successfully swapped out the value or the\n2100: \t\t// value stored in it is newer than newValue.\n2101: \t\tif value.CompareAndSwap(current, newValue) {\n2102: \t\t\treturn current, true\n2103: \t\t}\n2104: \t}\n2105: }\n2106: \n2107: func buildTimeSeries(timeSeries []prompb.TimeSeries, filter When shards are computed at the QueueManager level rather than the WriteStorage level, the `highestTimestamp` metric becomes stale during resharding transitions, causing the `calculateDesiredShards()` function to incorrectly calculate shard requirements and potentially stall when queues are full.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n**Location:** `storage/remote/queue_manager.go:1152-1224` (calculateDesiredShards function)\n\nThe root cause is a data collection and timing mismatch introduced when resharding logic moved timestamp tracking from WriteStorage-level to QueueManager-level:\n\n1. **Metric Location Change:** In commit 184c7eb, `highestTimestamp` moved from `metrics.highestTimestamp` (WriteStorage-wide) to `qm.highestRecvTimestamp` (per QueueManager). This per-queue metric is updated only when samples are **enqueued into the shard** (line 1380 in queue_manager.go).\n\n2. **Race Condition During Resharding:** When resharding occurs (shards.stop() → shards.start()), the following happens:\n   - Old shards are shut down, draining their queues\n   - New shards are created and started\n   - During this transition, **samples arriving at WriteStorage are temporarily routed to the old shard instances**\n   - The new QueueManager's `highestRecvTimestamp` doesn't reflect samples from the old shards because they were already enqueued to the old instance\n   - This creates a timestamp gap\n\n3. **Incorrect Shard Calculation:** In `calculateDesiredShards()` at lines 1167-1171:\n   ```go\n   highestRecv     = t.highestRecvTimestamp.Get()  // Per-queue metric, may be stale\n   highestSent     = t.metrics.highestSentTimestamp.Get()\n   delay           = highestRecv - highestSent\n   dataPending     = delay * dataInRate * dataKeptRatio\n   ```\n\n   If `highestRecv` is significantly behind `highestSent` (or zero after resharding), the `dataPending` calculation becomes invalid, leading to incorrect `desiredShards` calculation.\n\n4. **Deadlock/Stall Scenario:** When the queue is full and resharding is triggered:\n   - `shards.stop()` attempts to flush all data (line 1236)\n   - New incoming samples must be dropped or blocked\n   - But the resharding calculation is stale, preventing proper shard scaling\n   - This can cause a deadlock if `Append()` blocks while resharding is in progress\n\n## Evidence\n\n### Code References\n\n**File:** `storage/remote/queue_manager.go`\n\n1. **Line 1280-1308:** `shards.start()` creates new queues but doesn't inherit timestamp state from old shards:\n   ```go\n   func (s *shards) start(n int) {\n       s.mtx.Lock()\n       defer s.mtx.Unlock()\n\n       s.qm.metrics.pendingSamples.Set(0)  // Metrics reset\n       s.qm.metrics.numShards.Set(float64(n))\n\n       newQueues := make([]*queue, n)\n       for i := range n {\n           newQueues[i] = newQueue(...)\n       }\n       s.queues = newQueues  // Old queues discarded\n   ```\n\n2. **Line 1355-1382:** `enqueue()` is the only place where per-queue `highestTimestamp` is updated:\n   ```go\n   func (s *shards) enqueue(...) bool {\n       ...\n       s.qm.metrics.highestTimestamp.Set(float64(data.timestamp / 1000))  // Updated here only\n       return true\n   }\n   ```\n\n3. **Line 1152-1171:** `calculateDesiredShards()` uses the per-queue timestamp which becomes stale:\n   ```go\n   highestRecv     = t.highestRecvTimestamp.Get()  // Stale after resharding\n   highestSent     = t.metrics.highestSentTimestamp.Get()\n   delay           = highestRecv - highestSent     // Can be wrong or negative\n   ```\n\n4. **Line 1227-1241:** `reshardLoop()` stops old shards before starting new ones:\n   ```go\n   case numShards := <-t.reshardChan:\n       t.shards.stop()      // Old shards shut down\n       t.shards.start(numShards)  // New shards initialized\n   ```\n\n### Commit History\n\n- **184c7eb (2025-08-20):** \"storage/remote: compute highestTimestamp and dataIn at QueueManager level\" - Introduced the per-queue metric\n- **6806b68 (2025-10-29):** \"[release-3.7] fix: Remote-write: revert changes in the queue resharding logic\" - Reverted the problematic commit\n- **35c3232 (2025-11-14):** Marked the test `TestRemoteWrite_ReshardingWithoutDeadlock` as flaky due to deadlocks\n- **e799952 (2025-11-25):** Re-enabled the test with improved reliability\n\n### Test Evidence\n\nThe test at `cmd/prometheus/main_test.go:977-1050` (`TestRemoteWrite_ReshardingWithoutDeadlock`) was created specifically to catch this issue. It:\n- Creates a slow remote storage backend (1-second latency)\n- Limits queue capacity to force full queues\n- Monitors `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` to verify resharding succeeds\n- Previously failed due to deadlock when queues were full during resharding\n\n## Affected Components\n\n1. **storage/remote/queue_manager.go**\n   - `QueueManager.calculateDesiredShards()` - Uses stale timestamp metrics\n   - `shards.start()` - Doesn't preserve timestamp state across resharding\n   - `shards.enqueue()` - Only updates per-queue timestamp, not reflected in old shards\n\n2. **storage/remote/write.go**\n   - `timestampTracker.Commit()` - Increments global `samplesIn` but not per-queue metric during resharding\n\n3. **Metrics Affected**\n   - `prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_timestamp_seconds` - Per-queue, becomes stale\n   - `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` - Calculated from stale timestamp\n   - `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_pending` - Can become stuck if resharding stalls\n\n## Why the Issue is Intermittent\n\n1. **Timing-dependent:** The race only manifests when:\n   - Resharding is triggered (high throughput detected)\n   - Queue is near capacity\n   - Target discovery changes cause resharding (from the issue description)\n\n2. **Load-dependent:** The issue is more likely under:\n   - High sample arrival rate (pushes shards to capacity)\n   - Slow remote backend (high latency, low throughput)\n   - Frequent target discovery changes (frequent resharding attempts)\n\n3. **Not always detectable:** If samples trickle in slowly, the old shard metrics may update before the next calculation cycle, masking the issue.\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Fix Strategy\n\nThe revert in commit 6806b68 was the correct fix. The proper solution is:\n\n1. **Restore WriteStorage-level timestamp tracking** - Move `highestTimestamp` back to WriteStorage level (`timestampTracker.Commit()`) so it tracks all incoming samples before queue selection\n2. **Track dropped samples separately** - Maintain `dataDropped` counter for dropped-by-relabel samples separately from `dataIn`\n3. **Adjust shard calculation formula** - Include `dataKeptRatio` to account for relabeling:\n   ```go\n   dataKeptRatio = dataOutRate / (dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)\n   desiredShards = timePerSample * (dataInRate * dataKeptRatio + backlogCatchup)\n   ```\n\n### Diagnostic Steps\n\nTo confirm this is the root cause:\n\n1. **Check timestamp metrics during resharding:**\n   ```\n   prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_timestamp_seconds  # Should not reset to 0\n   prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_sent_timestamp_seconds  # Should advance\n   ```\n\n2. **Monitor reshape transitions:**\n   - Watch for log line: \"Remote storage resharding from=X to=Y\"\n   - Check if `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` increases during high load\n   - Verify samples flow continues during resharding\n\n3. **Load test scenarios:**\n   - Enable slow remote backend\n   - Set low queue capacity\n   - Force frequent target discovery changes\n   - Verify samples reach destination without stalling\n\n### Verification\n\nThe fix in commit 6806b68 successfully addresses this by:\n- Restoring per-WriteStorage `samplesIn` EWMA rate tracking\n- Adding per-WriteStorage `highestTimestamp` via `timestampTracker.Commit()`\n- Maintaining `dataDropped` separately for dropped samples\n- Running the `samplesIn.tick()` at WriteStorage level via goroutine\n- Passing both `samplesIn` and `highestTimestamp` to each QueueManager\n\nThis ensures resharding calculations use consistent, non-stale metrics across all queues.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":10029,"cache_read_input_tokens":62271,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":10029},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"68a6eef2-c842-4a6a-b008-bfe7e917893c","uuid":"404277f8-952b-4aa9-a7a2-069630da921d"}

Introduced the per-queue metric\n- **6806b68 (2025-10-29):** \"[release-3.7] fix: Remote-write: revert changes in the queue resharding logic\" - Reverted the problematic commit\n- **35c3232 (2025-11-14):** Marked the test `TestRemoteWrite_ReshardingWithoutDeadlock` as flaky due to deadlocks\n- **e799952 (2025-11-25):** Re-enabled the test with improved reliability\n\n### Test Evidence\n\nThe test at `cmd/prometheus/main_test.go:977-1050` (`TestRemoteWrite_ReshardingWithoutDeadlock`) was created specifically to catch this issue. It:\n- Creates a slow remote storage backend (1-second latency)\n- Limits queue capacity to force full queues\n- Monitors `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` to verify resharding succeeds\n- Previously failed due to deadlock when queues were full during resharding\n\n## Affected Components\n\n1. **storage/remote/queue_manager.go**\n   - `QueueManager.calculateDesiredShards()` - Uses stale timestamp metrics\n   - `shards.start()` - Doesn't preserve timestamp state across resharding\n   - `shards.enqueue()` - Only updates per-queue timestamp, not reflected in old shards\n\n2. **storage/remote/write.go**\n   - `timestampTracker.Commit()` - Increments global `samplesIn` but not per-queue metric during resharding\n\n3. **Metrics Affected**\n   - `prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_timestamp_seconds` - Per-queue, becomes stale\n   - `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` - Calculated from stale timestamp\n   - `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_pending` - Can become stuck if resharding stalls\n\n## Why the Issue is Intermittent\n\n1. **Timing-dependent:** The race only manifests when:\n   - Resharding is triggered (high throughput detected)\n   - Queue is near capacity\n   - Target discovery changes cause resharding (from the issue description)\n\n2. **Load-dependent:** The issue is more likely under:\n   - High sample arrival rate (pushes shards to capacity)\n   - Slow remote backend (high latency, low throughput)\n   - Frequent target discovery changes (frequent resharding attempts)\n\n3. **Not always detectable:** If samples trickle in slowly, the old shard metrics may update before the next calculation cycle, masking the issue.\n\n## Recommendation\n\n### Fix Strategy\n\nThe revert in commit 6806b68 was the correct fix. The proper solution is:\n\n1. **Restore WriteStorage-level timestamp tracking** - Move `highestTimestamp` back to WriteStorage level (`timestampTracker.Commit()`) so it tracks all incoming samples before queue selection\n2. **Track dropped samples separately** - Maintain `dataDropped` counter for dropped-by-relabel samples separately from `dataIn`\n3. **Adjust shard calculation formula** - Include `dataKeptRatio` to account for relabeling:\n   ```go\n   dataKeptRatio = dataOutRate / (dataDropped.rate() + dataOutRate)\n   desiredShards = timePerSample * (dataInRate * dataKeptRatio + backlogCatchup)\n   ```\n\n### Diagnostic Steps\n\nTo confirm this is the root cause:\n\n1. **Check timestamp metrics during resharding:**\n   ```\n   prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_timestamp_seconds  # Should not reset to 0\n   prometheus_remote_storage_queue_highest_sent_timestamp_seconds  # Should advance\n   ```\n\n2. **Monitor reshape transitions:**\n   - Watch for log line: \"Remote storage resharding from=X to=Y\"\n   - Check if `prometheus_remote_storage_shards_desired` increases during high load\n   - Verify samples flow continues during resharding\n\n3. **Load test scenarios:**\n   - Enable slow remote backend\n   - Set low queue capacity\n   - Force frequent target discovery changes\n   - Verify samples reach destination without stalling\n\n### Verification\n\nThe fix in commit 6806b68 successfully addresses this by:\n- Restoring per-WriteStorage `samplesIn` EWMA rate tracking\n- Adding per-WriteStorage `highestTimestamp` via `timestampTracker.Commit()`\n- Maintaining `dataDropped` separately for dropped samples\n- Running the `samplesIn.tick()` at WriteStorage level via goroutine\n- Passing both `samplesIn` and `highestTimestamp` to each QueueManager\n\nThis ensures resharding calculations use consistent, non-stale metrics across all queues.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Here's a summary of what I found:\n\n## Key Findings\n\n**Root Cause:** A race condition in the resharding logic introduced when timestamp tracking was moved from WriteStorage-level to QueueManager-level. When resharding occurs, the per-queue `highestTimestamp` metric becomes stale, causing incorrect shard calculations that can lead to deadlocks when queues are full.\n\n**Specific Mechanism:**\n1. Resharding moves `highestTimestamp` tracking to per-QueueManager level\n2. During resharding transitions, old shards shut down while new ones start up\n3. The new QueueManager doesn't reflect samples still in old shards\n4. Stale timestamp → incorrect delay calculation → wrong desired shard count\n5. When queue is full, this prevents proper scaling, causing stalls\n\n**Why It's Intermittent:**\n- Only occurs under specific conditions: high load + slow backend + frequent resharding\n- Depends on timing of samples arriving during shard transitions\n- Can be masked if samples arrive slowly enough to update metrics before next calculation\n\n**The Fix:** Commit 6806b68 correctly reverted the problematic changes and restored:\n- WriteStorage-level timestamp and sample rate tracking\n- Separate tracking of dropped (by relabeling) samples\n- Proper formula that accounts for data retention ratio\n\nThe investigation report has been written to `/logs/agent/investigation.md` with detailed evidence, code references, and diagnostic recommendations."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2427,"cache_read_input_tokens":72300,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2427},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"68a6eef2-c842-4a6a-b008-bfe7e917893c","uuid":"070c8633-1344-4efb-b0c8-f236eb10e6c3"}
